## [1.7.2] - 2026-09-01 (Volume workshop, targeted scrape, and auto-sync scan)

EN
### ⚠️ Good to know
This release is counted from **1.7.1**.
* **Auto-sync following a Kavita scan is opt-in.** Existing timer minutes are kept. An interval of `0` stays off. A positive interval stays on, still on a timer.
* **Volume enrichment stays off until you enable it.** Existing volume writes in Kavita are unchanged.
* **New lifetime stats start at zero.** Auto-sync waves, workshop counters, covers you pick and locks you seal start at zero on an existing library. They do not look backwards.

### ✨ What's new
* **C92. The /stats story catches up** — Chapters grow instead of hiding their cards. Inventory health, volume writes, Review gestures (merges, Super, weak picks) and the per-field map join the scroll. Manual covers now count the 🔒 mark. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **C93. /stats tells a few more stories** — Homemade titles, language tags, pardoned duplicates and forced expected counts join the scroll. Time saved also becomes TGV rides and nights of sleep; written volumes become a shelf in centimetres, and who wrote them is named. Still no new telemetry. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **C94. /stats starts counting how work arrives** — Covers you pick, locks you seal, and whether a successful write came from a batch, a webhook, auto-sync or a row click now live in lifetime counters. They start at zero on an existing library. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`, `tests/test_routes_series.py`, `tests/test_routes_series_cover.py`, `tests/test_sync_worker_resilience.py`, `tests/test_companion_webhook.py`.
* **C95. Diagnostics probes every scraper together** — Opening the page used to test only the Config cascade, one site after another. Every installed scraper now starts concurrently in a bounded pool of 4 workers with real-time streaming; per-site pacing is unchanged. “Test cascade” still re-runs only the Providers slots. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_diagnostics.py`.
* **C96. Auto-sync can follow a Kavita scan** — Config → Planning has a master switch, Auto / Review / Super for those jobs, and one trigger: every X minutes, or when a Kavita library scan finishes. Scan mode keeps an hours-long safety net if Meta was down. Stop also clears waiting Auto-sync jobs; a scrape already running finishes. Webhook and row clicks stay. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_config.py`, `tests/test_auto_sync_catchup.py`, `tests/test_kavita_hub_parse.py`, `tests/test_kavita_hub_live.py`.
* **C97. Auto-sync waves get a report** — When that Auto-sync queue finishes (not a dashboard batch), a teal button appears next to Manual Review with series / completed / error counts and the list of titles. Closing the modal marks the report read. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_report.py`.
* **C98. /stats has an Auto-sync chapter** — Closed Auto-sync waves (not dashboard batches) now count in lifetime: waves, series, completed, errors, Review, Stop, scan vs timer. They start at zero on an existing library. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`, `tests/test_auto_sync_report.py`.
* **C99. Dashboard filter for the last Auto-sync wave** — A “Latest wave” status filter (and Show in the list in the report) keeps only the series from that report. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_report.py`.
* **C100. Volume scrape follows the albums you own** — Manga-News, Bédéthèque and Planète BD only open owned volumes, and Stop is honoured in that loop. Hitting a provider cap no longer skips MangaDex covers; Stop during a covering text index keeps that index and does not then fetch MangaDex. Metron paginates its issue list. Bédéthèque albums carry ISBN. Manga-Sanctuary ranks just behind Manga-News once Magasin ships a volume index; openBD joins the ISBN cascade. Tests : `tests/test_volume_enrichment_providers.py`, `tests/test_volume_index_memo.py`, `tests/test_scraper_manganews_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_scraper_bd_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_scraper_metron_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_volume_provider_hints.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_cascade_flexible.py`.
* **C101. A volume workshop sits beside the inventory** — `/series/<id>/volumes` is its own page, like /stats. Opening it reads Kavita and Meta only — no scrape. Edit in place, Magic Input, Review, send overwrites filled and locked fields (an empty value, including localized name, is still never written; SKIPPED is 200). A library pass freezes only the series it is writing. Reset clears Meta and reloads Kavita, not a rewind; per-volume also reopens that unit for the automatic pass. 📑 stays inventory. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`, `tests/test_volume_unit_cache.py`, `tests/test_force_sync_queued.py`, `tests/test_playful_stats.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_ui.py`.
* **C102. The workshop rail filters like the dashboard, caches covers, and switches series without a reload** — Prefix / inside-title search, library, status and hide-ignored share the dashboard localStorage keys. Kavita covers are stored on disk and the browser may cache them. Clicking a series in the rail loads it beside the list. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C103. Workshop cards fold extra fields, the volume bar sticks, and the title opens Kavita** — Extra series and volume fields sit behind More. The volume bar stays on screen with a selected count. The series title opens the Kavita sheet. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C104. Empty fields glow, Magic Input stays in sight, and the live log sits beside the workshop** — Empty volume and series fields are amber. Magic Input is a labelled row. Prev/next grey out at the ends; `/` focuses the rail search. The gesture log names the volume. The live log sits beside the page. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C105. Workshop fields stay in order and short ones share a denser row** — Empty or filled, the sheet keeps the same order. Year, status, age and staff sit three to a row. Extra staff still sit behind More; the fold is remembered. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C106. Workshop buttons use the same names as the rest of MetaKavita** — The series card says Manual Review, Super Review and Reset. Volume Super says Super Review. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C107. Workshop cards are denser, and you can pick a cover** — Series and volume cards take less space. Covers look like books. **Pick** on a cover opens the live search; the URL is sent with Send, not written at once. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C108. Sovereign Workshop button on dashboard with custom SVG icon & teal design** — Workshop is no longer buried as an add-on inside the volume toolbar panel. It now sits prominently in the top navigation bar (always visible, sticky) and as a base action in the series toolbar header, with a bespoke SVG craft icon (`#mk-ico-workshop`) and modern teal theme styling. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_ui.py`.
* **C109. Workshop coffee support button & rare playful nagware** — The workshop top navigation bar now features a permanent amber Buy Me a Coffee button (☕) with hover micro-interaction, instant toast feedback, and auto-cooldown registration. The rare, polite supporter nagware overlay (`_license_nag_modal.html`) is also available in the workshop with a dedicated `workshop_craft` variant triggered after successful writes to Kavita, following the 7-day honeymoon, minimum activity threshold of 10 items, max 1–2/day, 30-day honor snooze, and 7-day BMC click cooldown safeguards. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_coffee.py`, `tests/test_supporter_nag_policy.py`.
* **C110. Inventory deletion resilience, volumetric duplicates & Kavita scan trigger** — Addresses Kavita's deletion limitations. Duplicate groups display real volume/chapter counts, a “🌟 Recommended” badge and a safety confirmation against trashing richer copies. Adds native PowerShell (.ps1) script generation, a “⚡ Trigger Kavita Scan” button to update Kavita directly from MetaKavita, “Mark as resolved” for duplicates, 1-click purge of empty series shells (0 vols) with surgical cache clearing, auto-cache pruning on scans, SignalR `SeriesRemoved` handling, and quick series exclusion in Missing Volumes. Tests : `tests/test_dup_folder_script.py`, `tests/test_library_audit.py`.
* **C111. Persistent volume drafts and multi-scraper candidate gathering** — Editing any field on a volume card in the workshop automatically debounces and saves a persistent draft in SQLite (`/draft-volume`), tagged as staged so that concurrent automatic library passes will not overwrite user input. Volume review by ISBN or title queries all active scrapers in parallel (`all_scrapers=True`) instead of stopping at the first match, presenting the user with an exhaustive choice of candidate editions. Staged edits and dirty counters persist across reloads. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_volume_fixes.py`, `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`.
* **C112. Modern, high-density & compact Workshop CSS redesign** — Overhaul of workshop styles (`volumes.css`). Information density is doubled: series card height is halved (124×186 px cover, 26–28 px inputs), revealing volumes without scrolling. Volume cards shrink from ~450 px to ~190–210 px with prominent 104×156 px covers, inline Champ Magique and 24 px inputs. Soft amber glow for empty fields, dark glassmorphism bar, 48 px rail items syncing virtual scroll and unblocking volume shortcuts. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`, `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`.
* **C113. Series-to-volumes cascade, modern cards, seamless covers & live send logs** — Workshop cards adopt the sleek look of Manual Review: modern slate #141926 background, 8px corners, sentence-case labels and gentle amber empty tints. 1-click series-to-volumes cascade (`.workshop-cascade-btn`) duplicates metadata down to all volumes, with a global button (`#workshopCascadeAllBtn`). Status chips on volume cards. Series and volume covers integrate seamlessly beside headers and top fields with lower fields flowing full-width beneath them without dead space. Live WebSocket log line on every volume, selection, and series send to Kavita. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`, `tests/test_workshop.py`.

### 🐛 Bug Fixes
* **BF174. The /stats page shows every library state** — Since 1.7.0 the dashboard counts six states, including Needs seal and Manual Review. The detailed stats page still drew four slices on a six-number chart, so the donut was wrong and two states were missing. They are back, with the same names as the sidebar. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF175. Stats chapters no longer clip their own mosaics** — Each chapter was a viewport with `overflow: hidden`. Once the page grew past four tiles, the rest was cut off and the scroll jumped to the next beat. Chapters now grow; the story scrolls again. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF176. /stats is its own page, not a dashboard pane** — The detailed stats page reused the dashboard shell (`dashboard-wrapper` / `content`). Those classes lock the viewport and hide overflow, so the story looked gutted even when the HTML was there. /stats now loads its own CSS and JS after the app stylesheet, on a document that can scroll. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF177. The average manual-review score stays between 0 and 100 %** — The tile could read 1200 %: scored confirms started counting after the lifetime sum, so a handful of new reviews divided years of scores. Tests : `tests/test_manual_review.py`, `tests/test_mr_achievements.py`.
* **BF178. Volume time is typing, not reading** — The tile counted 25 minutes of reading per written volume. Filling three fields is not reading a book. It now counts about a minute of paste per volume. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF179. /stats no longer mixes two clocks** — Lifetime tiles reused cache captions (mysteries still in the library, current-cache hit rate, provider-win total on “matches / series”). “Manga pages” counted 160 pages per series, as if a Kavita series were one tankobon. A surgical field mask that omitted cover was counted as a protected 🔒. Time saved now includes the minute of paste per written volume; pages come from written volumes (~180 each); the lifetime hit rate stays 0 until telemetry exists. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF180. Planète BD search keeps the query** — The site moved search to `/recherche.html`. The old `/recherche/?mot-clef=` URL redirected there *without* the keyword, so every search looked empty. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_http_cadence.py`.
* **BF181. Fandom diagnostics no longer probe www.fandom.com** — That homepage is Cloudflare-blocked, so the health check marked Fandom down before asking a wiki. It now hits a wiki API and skips the empty `fetch_covers`. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_diagnostics.py`.
* **BF182. Babelio searches via sitemaps, not the homepage** — The HTML search and homepage now serve a 403 captcha. `babmap_1.xml`–`babmap_37.xml` and book pages still answer. Search reads those sitemaps (disk cache, one file every 3 s until a match); warmup is gone. An ISBN does not crawl the 37 maps. Diagnostics probes `babmap_1.xml` then a known book URL. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_babelio_sitemap.py`.
* **BF183. Saving Config shows a toast** — The modal Save button gave no visible confirmation. A short toast now confirms the write. Tests : `tests/test_frontend_dom_ids.py`.
* **BF184. Config Save is not blocked by a leftover Auto-sync 0** — The minutes field had `min="1"`. Native validation ran before `saveConfig`, so Save did nothing (no reload, no toast) while Auto-sync was off and the field still held `0`. `0` is allowed unless the switch is on and the trigger is minutes. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_config.py`.
* **BF185. Saving Config no longer reloads the page** — The Save button wrote then reloaded, which wiped scroll, filters and the open modal. It now stays put and the toast appears on the modal. Changing the interface language still reloads: every label is rendered on the server. Tests : `tests/test_frontend_dom_ids.py`.
* **BF186. The left sidebar no longer unrolls on load** — Options started closed in the HTML and `main.js` opened it after every script had run. The details-content transition then played a 0.28 s unroll. The panel is open in the markup, restored during parse, and that transition is off until the first paint. Tests : `tests/test_sidebar_boot.py`.
* **BF187. The left sidebar remembers how you left it** — Open/closed panels and the scroll offset are written to `localStorage` (toggle, scroll, leaving the page) and re-applied after the first paint, so a queued `toggle` from the markup `open` cannot overwrite them. Tests : `tests/test_sidebar_boot.py`.
* **BF195. Inventory fixes, health bar preservation, and config safety** — Duplicate folder paths in /save-config use a dedicated partial branch to avoid wiping KAVITA_URL and settings. Dismissed duplicates update counts and share across libraries. The health bar and count chips stay visible during duplicate reviews. Esc closes modals sequentially, freshness translates the day unit, ComicVine cleans '4050-' prefixes, AniList allows title search with other IDs, 404s handle missing series, and badges keep colors when hydrated. Tests : `tests/test_library_audit.py`.

### 🏗️ Internal maintenance
* **CSS refactor (no C/BF code)** — The single `style.css` sheet (6 864 lines) was split into **21 focused modules** grouped under `static/css/base/`, `layout/`, `components/` and `modals/`. `style.css` becomes a 21-`@import` manifest. No visual rendering was changed. 204 lines of confirmed dead CSS (26 orphaned classes: old duplicate buttons, unused card tints, V1 vol-preview component, toolbar leftovers, log-modifiers, abandoned MR/so-field/config-grid rules) were removed before the split. A bit-for-bit backup is kept in `static/css/style.backup.css`.
* **Workshop CSS refactor (no C/BF code)** — The 1 578-line `volumes.css` sheet was split into **14 focused modules** in `static/css/workshop/` (_base, _navbar, _layout, _rail, _main, _series-card, _covers, _forms, _action-bar, _volume-cards, _candidates, _logs, _modals, _responsive). `volumes.css` becomes a 14-`@import` manifest. No visual rendering was changed. Bit-for-bit backup in `static/css/volumes.backup.css`. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`.

### ⚠️ À savoir
Cette version est comptée depuis la **1.7.1**.
* **L'auto-sync suivant un scan Kavita est un choix.** Les minutes déjà choisies sont gardées. Un intervalle à `0` reste éteint. Un intervalle positif reste allumé, toujours sur minuterie.
* **L'enrichissement par tome reste éteint tant que tu ne l'allumes pas.** Les écritures de tomes déjà dans Kavita ne changent pas.
* **Les nouvelles stats partent de zéro.** Vagues d'auto-sync, compteurs de l'atelier, couvertures posées et 🔒 scellés partent de zéro sur une bibliothèque existante. Elles ne regardent pas en arrière.

### ✨ Nouveautés
* **C92. Le récit /stats rattrape** — Les chapitres grandissent au lieu de cacher leurs cartes. Santé de l'Inventaire, écritures de tomes, gestes Review (fusions, Super, picks faibles) et la carte par champ rejoignent le scroll. Les couvertures manuelles comptent le 🔒. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **C93. /stats raconte encore un peu** — Titres maison, langues, doublons pardonnés et attendus forcés rejoignent le scroll. Le temps gagné devient aussi des TGV et des nuits de sommeil ; les tomes écrits, une étagère en centimètres, et on nomme qui les a remplis. Toujours pas de nouvelle télémétrie. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **C94. /stats commence à compter d'où vient le travail** — Les couvertures que tu poses, les 🔒 que tu scelles, et si une écriture réussie vient d'un lot, d'un webhook, de l'auto-sync ou d'un clic ligne vivent maintenant dans des compteurs lifetime. Ils partent de zéro sur une bibliothèque déjà en place. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`, `tests/test_routes_series.py`, `tests/test_routes_series_cover.py`, `tests/test_sync_worker_resilience.py`, `tests/test_companion_webhook.py`.
* **C95. Le diagnostic sonde tous les scrapers ensemble** — Ouvrir la page ne testait que la cascade Config, un site après l'autre. Chaque scraper installé part maintenant en parallèle dans un pool léger de 4 workers avec flux en temps réel ; la cadence par site ne change pas. « Tester la cascade » ne rejoue que les slots Providers. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_diagnostics.py`.
* **C96. L'auto-sync peut suivre un scan Kavita** — Config → Planification a un interrupteur maître, Auto / Review / Super pour ces jobs, et un seul déclencheur : toutes les X minutes, ou à la fin d'un scan de bibliothèque Kavita. Le mode scan garde un filet en heures si Meta était éteint. Stop vide aussi l'Auto-sync en attente ; le scrape déjà lancé continue. Webhook et clic ligne restent. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_config.py`, `tests/test_auto_sync_catchup.py`, `tests/test_kavita_hub_parse.py`, `tests/test_kavita_hub_live.py`.
* **C97. L'auto-sync a un rapport de vague** — Quand cette file Auto-sync se termine (pas un lot du tableau de bord), un bouton sarcelle apparaît à côté des Reviews, avec le nombre de séries, les terminées, les erreurs, et la liste. Le fermer marque le rapport lu. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_report.py`.
* **C98. /stats a un chapitre Auto-sync** — Les vagues Auto-sync closes (pas les lots du tableau de bord) comptent maintenant en lifetime : vagues, séries, terminées, erreurs, Review, Stop, scan vs minuterie. Ils partent de zéro sur une bibliothèque déjà en place. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`, `tests/test_auto_sync_report.py`.
* **C99. Filtre du tableau de bord pour la dernière vague Auto-sync** — Un filtre « Dernière vague » (et Voir dans la liste dans le rapport) ne garde que les séries de ce rapport. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_report.py`.
* **C100. Le scrape des tomes suit les albums que tu as** — Manga-News, Bédéthèque et Planète BD n'ouvrent que les tomes possédés, et Stop est honoré dans cette boucle. Toucher le plafond d'un fournisseur ne saute plus les jaquettes MangaDex ; Stop pendant un index textuel assez couvrant le garde et ne part pas ensuite chercher MangaDex. Metron pagine sa liste d'issues. Les fiches Bédéthèque portent un ISBN. Manga-Sanctuary se place juste derrière Manga-News dès que le Magasin livre l'index ; openBD rejoint la cascade ISBN. Tests : `tests/test_volume_enrichment_providers.py`, `tests/test_volume_index_memo.py`, `tests/test_scraper_manganews_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_scraper_bd_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_scraper_metron_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_volume_provider_hints.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_cascade_flexible.py`.
* **C101. Un atelier des tomes à côté de l'inventaire** — `/series/<id>/volumes` est une page à part, comme /stats. L'ouvrir lit Kavita et Meta seulement — aucun scrape. Édition, Champ Magique, Review, envoi en force (écrase remplis et verrous ; une valeur vide, nom localisé compris, n'est jamais écrite ; SKIPPED = 200). Une passe ne gèle que la série qu'elle écrit. Reset = Meta + relecture, pas de rewind ; un tome rouvre aussi l'unité pour la passe. 📑 reste l'inventaire. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`, `tests/test_volume_unit_cache.py`, `tests/test_force_sync_queued.py`, `tests/test_playful_stats.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_ui.py`.
* **C102. Le rail de l'atelier filtre comme le tableau de bord, garde les jaquettes, et change de série sans recharger** — Recherche (préfixe / dans le titre), bibliothèque, statut et masquer les ignorés partagent les clés localStorage du tableau de bord. Les jaquettes Kavita sont stockées sur disque et le navigateur peut les garder. Un clic dans le rail charge la série à côté. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C103. L'atelier plie les champs en trop, la barre tomes reste à l'écran, le titre ouvre Kavita** — Les champs secondaires de la fiche et des tomes se rangent sous Plus. La barre des tomes reste visible avec le compte de sélection. Le titre ouvre la fiche Kavita. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C104. Les vides se voient, le Champ Magique reste sous les yeux, le journal live est à côté** — Les champs vides de la fiche et des tomes sont ambre. Le Champ Magique a un vrai libellé. ← → se grisent en bout de liste ; `/` pose le focus sur la recherche. Le journal de gestes nomme le tome. Le journal live est à côté. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C105. Les champs gardent leur ordre, les courts se serrent** — Vides ou remplis, la fiche garde le même ordre. Année, statut, âge et staff se mettent à trois par ligne. Le staff en trop reste sous Plus ; le volet est mémorisé. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C106. Les boutons de l'atelier ont les mêmes noms que le reste de MetaKavita** — La fiche dit Manual Review, Super Review et Reset. Le Super d'un tome dit Super Review. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C107. Les cartes de l'atelier sont plus denses, et on choisit une jaquette** — Les cartes fiche et tome prennent moins de place. Les jaquettes ont un relief de livre. **Choisir** sur une jaquette ouvre la recherche live ; l'URL part à l'envoi, pas tout de suite. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`.
* **C108. Bouton Atelier souverain sur le tableau de bord avec icône SVG dédiée & design sarcelle** — L'Atelier n'est plus enfermé comme un sous-élément du panneau de volume. Il trône désormais dans la barre d'en-tête principale (sticky, toujours accessible) ainsi que dans l'en-tête de la barre d'outils des séries, paré d'une icône SVG sur-mesure (`#mk-ico-workshop`) et d'un design thématique sarcelle/teal soigné. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_ui.py`.
* **C109. Bouton café permanent et nagware supporter dans l'Atelier** — La barre de navigation supérieure de l'Atelier intègre désormais un bouton ambré permanent « M'offrir un café » (☕) avec micro-interaction au survol, toast immédiat et enregistrement du cooldown. L'overlay nagware supporter (`_license_nag_modal.html`) est également intégré à l'Atelier avec une variante dédiée `workshop_craft` déclenchée après écritures réussies dans Kavita, dans le respect strict des gardes-fous (lune de miel 7 j, seuil d'activité minimum de 10 items, max 1–2/j, silence honneur 30 j, cooldown clic BMC 7 j). Tests : `tests/test_workshop_coffee.py`, `tests/test_supporter_nag_policy.py`.
* **C110. Résilience des suppressions, comparatif volumétrique et scan Kavita** — Répond aux limites de suppression de Kavita. Les doublons affichent les tomes/chapitres réels, un badge « 🌟 Recommandé » et une alerte bloquant la suppression d'une copie plus riche. Ajout de scripts PowerShell (.ps1), d'un bouton « ⚡ Déclencher le scan Kavita » pour actualiser Kavita depuis MetaKavita, d'une action « Marquer comme traité », de la purge en 1 clic des séries vides (0 tome) avec nettoyage chirurgical du cache, du nettoyage d'orphelins au scan, du suivi SignalR `SeriesRemoved` et de l'exclusion rapide dans les Manquants. Tests : `tests/test_dup_folder_script.py`, `tests/test_library_audit.py`.
* **C111. Brouillons de tomes persistants et collecte multi-fournisseurs** — La saisie sur une carte de tome en Atelier sauvegarde automatiquement en tâche de fond (debounced 500 ms) un brouillon persistant en SQLite (`/draft-volume`), tagué stagé pour empêcher la passe automatique concurrente d'écraser le travail en cours. La review de tome par ISBN ou titre interroge désormais l'ensemble des scrapers actifs (`all_scrapers=True`) au lieu d'interrompre la recherche au premier résultat, offrant un choix exhaustif de candidats. Les tomes stagés et l'état dirty persistent au rechargement. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_volume_fixes.py`, `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`.
* **C112. Refonte CSS moderne, haute densité et ergonomique de l'Atelier** — Refonte des styles de l'Atelier (`volumes.css`). Densité d'information doublée : hauteur de fiche divisée par deux (jaquette 124×186 px, inputs 26–28 px), rendant les tomes visibles sans scroll. Cartes de tomes allégées de ~450 px à ~190–210 px avec jaquettes valorisées 104×156 px et inputs 24 px. Liseré ambre doux pour champs vides (`rgba(251, 191, 36, 0.38)`), barre en dark glassmorphism, rail 48 px calant le scroll virtuel et débloquant les raccourcis tome. Tests : `tests/test_workshop.py`, `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`.
* **C113. Duplication série -> tomes, cartes modernes, couvertures fluides & logs d'envoi** — L'Atelier adopte le design de la Revue Manuelle : fond ardoise #141926, coins 8px, casse naturelle et liseré ambre doux. Micro-bouton de duplication unitaire (`.workshop-cascade-btn`) et bouton global (`#workshopCascadeAllBtn`) cascadant les métadonnées vers les tomes. Puces de statut sur les tomes. Couvertures intégrées aux côtés des en-têtes avec métadonnées inférieures en pleine largeur sans vide. Ligne de journalisation WebSocket de succès émise à chaque envoi de tome, sélection ou série vers Kavita. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`, `tests/test_workshop.py`.

### 🐛 Correctifs
* **BF174. La page /stats montre tous les états de la bibliothèque** — Depuis la 1.7.0 le tableau de bord compte six états, dont À sceller et Review. La page détaillée dessinait encore quatre parts sur un graphique à six nombres : le donut était faux et deux états manquaient. Ils sont de retour, sous les mêmes noms que la barre latérale. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF175. Les chapitres de stats ne coupent plus leurs mosaïques** — Chaque chapitre était un viewport avec `overflow: hidden`. Dès que la page dépassait quatre tuiles, le reste était coupé et le scroll sautait au chapitre suivant. Les chapitres grandissent ; le récit scrolle à nouveau. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF176. /stats est une page à elle, pas un volet du tableau de bord** — La page détaillée réutilisait la coquille du dashboard (`dashboard-wrapper` / `content`). Ces classes figent la hauteur de l'écran et masquent le débordement : le récit avait l'air vidé alors que le HTML était là. /stats charge maintenant sa propre feuille et son JS après celle de l'app, sur un document qui peut scroller. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF177. Le score moyen de la review manuelle reste entre 0 et 100 %** — La tuile pouvait afficher 1200 % : les confirms scorées ont commencé à compter après la somme lifetime, donc une poignée de reviews récentes divisait des années de scores. Tests : `tests/test_manual_review.py`, `tests/test_mr_achievements.py`.
* **BF178. Le temps des tomes est de la saisie, pas de la lecture** — La tuile comptait 25 min de lecture par tome écrit. Remplir trois champs, ce n'est pas lire un livre. Elle compte maintenant environ une minute de collage par tome. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF179. /stats ne mélange plus deux horloges** — Les tuiles lifetime réutilisaient des libellés du cache (mystères encore dans la bibliothèque, taux de hit du cache actuel, victoires providers sur « matchs / série »). « Pages manga » comptait 160 pages par série, comme si une série Kavita était un tankobon. Un masque chirurgical sans cover était compté comme une couverture 🔒. Le temps gagné inclut maintenant la minute de collage par tome écrit ; les pages viennent des tomes écrits (~180 chacune) ; le taux de hit lifetime reste à 0 tant que la télémétrie n'a pas démarré. Tests : `tests/test_playful_stats.py`.
* **BF180. La recherche Planète BD garde le mot-clé** — Le site a déplacé la recherche vers `/recherche.html`. L'ancienne URL `/recherche/?mot-clef=` y redirigeait *sans* le mot-clé : toute recherche paraissait vide. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_http_cadence.py`.
* **BF181. Le diagnostic Fandom ne sonde plus www.fandom.com** — Cette homepage est derrière Cloudflare : le health-check marquait Fandom down avant d'interroger un wiki. Il tape maintenant une API wiki et ignore le `fetch_covers` vide. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_diagnostics.py`.
* **BF182. Babelio cherche via les sitemaps, plus la homepage** — La recherche HTML et la homepage servent un captcha 403. `babmap_1.xml`–`babmap_37.xml` et les fiches livre répondent encore. La recherche lit ces sitemaps (cache disque, une carte toutes les 3 s jusqu'au match) ; plus de warmup. Un ISBN ne tire pas les 37 cartes. Le diagnostic sonde `babmap_1.xml` puis une fiche connue. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_babelio_sitemap.py`.
* **BF183. Sauvegarder la Config affiche un toast** — Le bouton de la modale ne confirmait rien à l'écran. Un toast confirme maintenant l'écriture. Tests : `tests/test_frontend_dom_ids.py`.
* **BF184. Sauvegarder la Config n'est plus bloqué par un 0 d'Auto-sync hérité** — Le champ minutes avait `min="1"`. La validation native partait avant `saveConfig` : Sauvegarder ne faisait rien (pas de rechargement, pas de toast) alors que l'auto-sync était éteint et que le champ valait encore `0`. `0` est permis sauf si l'interrupteur est allumé et le déclencheur est les minutes. Tests : `tests/test_auto_sync_config.py`.
* **BF185. Sauvegarder la Config ne recharge plus la page** — Le bouton écrivait puis rechargeait : scroll, filtres et modale ouverts disparaissaient. Il reste en place, le toast s'affiche sur la modale. Changer la langue d'interface recharge encore : chaque libellé est rendu côté serveur. Tests : `tests/test_frontend_dom_ids.py`.
* **BF186. La barre de gauche ne se déroule plus au chargement** — Options partait fermé dans le HTML et `main.js` l'ouvrait après tous les scripts. La transition details-content jouait alors un déroulement de 0,28 s. Le panneau est ouvert dans le gabarit, restauré pendant l'analyse, et cette transition est coupée jusqu'au premier paint. Tests : `tests/test_sidebar_boot.py`.
* **BF187. La barre de gauche se souvient de comment tu l'as laissée** — Panneaux ouverts/fermés et défilement sont écrits dans `localStorage` (toggle, scroll, départ de la page) et réappliqués après le premier paint, pour qu'un `toggle` en file du `open` HTML ne les écrase pas. Tests : `tests/test_sidebar_boot.py`.
* **BF195. Sécurisation de la config, barre de santé et correctifs inventaire** — La sauvegarde des dossiers de doublons utilise un embranchement partiel dédié évitant d'écraser KAVITA_URL et les réglages. Les doublons ignorés actualisent les compteurs et se partagent avec la vue globale. La barre de santé et les compteurs ne disparaissent plus à l'ouverture des doublons. Échap empile les modales proprement, la fraîcheur traduit les jours, ComicVine nettoie '4050-', AniList autorise le titre sans son ID, et les cartouches gardent leur couleur. Tests : `tests/test_library_audit.py`.

### 🏗️ Maintenance interne
* **Refactor CSS (sans code C/BF)** — L'unique feuille `style.css` (6 864 lignes) a été découpée en **21 modules ciblés** sous `static/css/base/`, `layout/`, `components/` et `modals/`. `style.css` devient un manifest de 21 `@import`. Aucun rendu visuel n'a été modifié. 204 lignes de code mort (26 classes orphelines : boutons doublons, teintes inutilisées, reliquats de toolbar, composant vol-preview V1, classes abandonnées) ont été purgées. Sauvegarde bit-à-bit conservée dans `static/css/style.backup.css`.
* **Refactor CSS de l'Atelier (sans code C/BF)** — La feuille `volumes.css` (1 578 lignes) a été découpée en **14 modules ciblés** dans `static/css/workshop/` (_base, _navbar, _layout, _rail, _main, _series-card, _covers, _forms, _action-bar, _volume-cards, _candidates, _logs, _modals, _responsive). `volumes.css` devient un manifest de 14 `@import`. Aucun rendu visuel n'a été modifié. Sauvegarde bit-à-bit dans `static/css/volumes.backup.css`. Tests : `tests/test_workshop_audit_fixes.py`.

## [1.7.1] - 2026-08-28 (A provider per field, a readable live log, Manga-News finds the French record)

EN
### ⚠️ Good to know
This release is counted from **1.7.0**.
* **Auto keeps working as it did.** The new per-field map is off and hidden until you turn it on. Nothing in a batch changes until you do.
* **The Inventory no longer deletes a duplicate from Kavita.** That button only removed the record. The files stayed on disk, the next scan put the series back. You now copy the folder path and, if you want, download a script to move or delete those files yourself. MetaKavita never runs that script.

### ✨ What's new
* **C88. Auto: choose who fills each field ([#38](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/38))** — Save a map per library type: a default source (the usual cascade, or one site) and, if you want, exceptions — covers always from AniList, credits always from MyAnimeList. Each field comes from one place, not a mix. Off and hidden until you turn it on. A forced provider ignores the map; without Force update, Kavita is left alone. Tests : `tests/test_field_assembly.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping_config.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping_fetch.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping_enrich.py`, `tests/test_skip_keys.py`, `tests/test_manual_completion_field_picks.py`.
* **C86. Manual Review: take a field from another card, or combine the lists ([#38](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/38))** — **Manual completion** puts a box on every field of every card: AniList's cover, MAL's credits, MangaBaka's publisher — one winner per field. **Merge fields** adds tags, genres and credits from several cards together; duplicates stay visible so you can tidy them before send. Auto Smart Completion does not change. Tests : `tests/test_manual_completion_field_picks.py`, `tests/test_frontend_manual_completion.py`.
* **C87. Adjust before send: choose what Kavita receives** — Each field on the recap has a tick, on by default. Untick it and Kavita is left as it is — the value is not cleared. If that series' Options dropped a field, the box is locked. Tests : `tests/test_mr_send_fields.py`.
* **C85. Duplicates: copy the folder, run a script** — Kavita delete is gone. Each extra copy shows its folder, with **Copy**. Tick **Trash**, then copy or download a script that moves the files — or deletes them if you choose. If Kavita's paths are not the disk's (Docker, a NAS), add a **Folder path prefix** at the bottom of that window. Keep the trash folder outside your libraries or the scan will ingest it. Tests : `tests/test_dup_folder_script.py`.
* **C90. The live log is readable** — Same type as the rest of the app: time on the left, the series named once, a coloured rail on errors. **Pause** (or scroll up) stops the jump; **Clear** empties the pane. Tests : `tests/test_live_logs_ui.py`.
* **C84. The live log names the series** — Every line reads `« One Piece » (5605)` — title first, Kavita id beside it. When there is no title: `« series 5605 »`. Tests : `tests/test_volume_enrichment_journal.py`, `tests/test_routes_series_cover.py`, `tests/test_sync_queue_priority.py`.
* **C89. A chip when a series has its own settings** — Forced provider, Magic ID, a field mask that is no longer everything, an alternative title, a publisher preference, language tags on the localised title: each now has a chip. Click it to open Options. Tests : `tests/test_override_panel_ui.py`, `tests/test_batch_targeted_fields.py`.
* **C91. Manual Review and Super Review fill in sooner** — With Smart Scoring off, the cards used to arrive one site after another. They now arrive together. Auto still processes one series at a time. Tests : `tests/test_field_mapping_fetch.py`, `tests/test_metadata_fetcher_smart_scoring.py`.

### 🐛 Bug Fixes
* **BF171. Manga-News finds the French record from an English Kavita title** — Kavita often stores the English name (`Delicious in Dungeon`, `Attack on Titan`). Known titles now retry as their French names (`Gloutons et Dragons`, `L'Attaque des Titans`, …). A mixed English/French volume list no longer comes back empty. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_manganews_volume_index.py`.
* **BF173. ComicVine no longer picks the year next door ([#40](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/40))** — On Batman, Avengers, Superman, ComicVine could prefer a neighbour with more issues over the year you asked for. The year you asked for now wins. The ComicVine scraper updates on the next start. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_comicvine_scoring_et_erreurs.py`, `tests/test_scraper_comicvine_issue_pass.py`.
* **BF172. Confirming a review now sends the cover you see** — With Auto cover off, confirming left Kavita's old cover in place even though the recap already showed the new one. Confirming now sends that cover. Tests : `tests/test_manual_review.py`.
* **Companion 1.0.28 — Test connection no longer says “failed” when the webhook token is empty ([#37](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/37))** — An empty token was reported as a network failure, and Test could overwrite a token already saved. The panel now asks you to paste the token from MetaKavita → Configuration.

FR
### ⚠️ À savoir
Cette version est comptée depuis la **1.7.0**.
* **L'Auto continue comme avant.** La nouvelle carte par champ est éteinte et masquée tant que vous ne l'allumez pas. Rien ne change dans un lot tant que vous ne le faites pas.
* **L'Inventaire ne supprime plus un doublon dans Kavita.** Ce bouton n'enlevait que la fiche. Les fichiers restaient sur le disque, le scan suivant remettait la série. Vous copiez maintenant le chemin du dossier et, si vous voulez, vous téléchargez un script pour déplacer ou supprimer ces fichiers vous-même. MetaKavita n'exécute jamais ce script.

### ✨ Nouveautés
* **C88. Auto : choisir qui remplit chaque champ ([#38](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/38))** — Enregistrez une carte par type de bibliothèque : une source par défaut (la cascade, ou un site) et, si vous voulez, des exceptions — couvertures toujours AniList, crédits toujours MyAnimeList. Chaque champ vient d'un seul endroit. Éteinte et masquée tant que vous ne l'allumez pas. Un fournisseur forcé ignore la carte ; sans Force update, Kavita n'est pas écrasé. Tests : `tests/test_field_assembly.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping_config.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping_fetch.py`, `tests/test_field_mapping_enrich.py`, `tests/test_skip_keys.py`, `tests/test_manual_completion_field_picks.py`.
* **C86. Review manuelle : prendre un champ sur une autre carte, ou combiner les listes ([#38](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/38))** — **Complétion manuelle** pose une case sur chaque champ de chaque carte : la couverture AniList, les crédits MAL, l'éditeur MangaBaka — un gagnant par champ. **Fusionner les champs** additionne tags, genres et crédits de plusieurs cartes ; les doublons restent visibles pour que vous puissiez les ranger avant l'envoi. La Complétion intelligente de l'Auto ne change pas. Tests : `tests/test_manual_completion_field_picks.py`, `tests/test_frontend_manual_completion.py`.
* **C87. Ajuster avant envoi : choisir ce que Kavita reçoit** — Chaque champ du récap a une case, cochée par défaut. Décochez : Kavita n'est pas touché, on ne vide pas. Si les Options de la série ont retiré ce champ, la case est verrouillée. Tests : `tests/test_mr_send_fields.py`.
* **C85. Doublons : copier le dossier, lancer un script** — Plus de suppression Kavita. Chaque copie montre son dossier, avec **Copier**. Cochez **Jeter**, puis copiez ou téléchargez un script qui déplace les fichiers — ou les supprime si vous le choisissez. Si les chemins Kavita ne sont pas ceux du disque (Docker, un NAS), ajoutez un **Préfixe des chemins** en bas de cette fenêtre. La corbeille doit rester hors de vos bibliothèques, sinon le scan l'ingère. Tests : `tests/test_dup_folder_script.py`.
* **C90. Le journal live se lit** — Même police que le reste de l'app : l'heure à gauche, la série nommée une fois, un liséré sur les erreurs. **Pause** (ou défiler vers le haut) arrête le saut ; **Vider** vide le volet. Tests : `tests/test_live_logs_ui.py`.
* **C84. Le journal nomme la série** — Chaque ligne se lit `« One Piece » (5605)` — le titre d'abord, l'identifiant Kavita à côté. S'il n'y a pas de titre : `« série 5605 »`. Tests : `tests/test_volume_enrichment_journal.py`, `tests/test_routes_series_cover.py`, `tests/test_sync_queue_priority.py`.
* **C89. Une pastille quand une série a ses propres réglages** — Fournisseur forcé, ID magique, masque de champs, titre alternatif, préférence d'éditeur, langues du titre localisé : une pastille sur la ligne. Un clic ouvre les Options. Tests : `tests/test_override_panel_ui.py`, `tests/test_batch_targeted_fields.py`.
* **C91. La Review manuelle et la Super Review se remplissent plus tôt** — Avec le Smart Scoring éteint, les cartes arrivaient un site après l'autre. Elles arrivent maintenant ensemble. L'Auto traite toujours une série à la fois. Tests : `tests/test_field_mapping_fetch.py`, `tests/test_metadata_fetcher_smart_scoring.py`.

### 🐛 Correctifs
* **BF171. Manga-News retrouve la fiche VF à partir d'un titre anglais Kavita** — Kavita stocke souvent le nom anglais (`Delicious in Dungeon`, `Attack on Titan`). Les titres connus sont relancés sous leur nom français (`Gloutons et Dragons`, `L'Attaque des Titans`, …). Une liste de tomes mêlée EN/VF ne revient plus vide. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_manganews_volume_index.py`.
* **BF173. ComicVine ne choisit plus l'année d'à côté ([#40](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/40))** — Sur Batman, Avengers, Superman, ComicVine préférait parfois un voisin plus fourni à l'année demandée. L'année demandée gagne. Le scraper ComicVine se met à jour au prochain démarrage. Tests : `tests/test_scraper_comicvine_scoring_et_erreurs.py`, `tests/test_scraper_comicvine_issue_pass.py`.
* **BF172. Confirmer une review envoie maintenant la couverture que vous voyez** — Avec Couverture auto éteinte, confirmer laissait l'ancienne couverture de Kavita alors que le récap montrait déjà la nouvelle. Confirmer envoie désormais cette couverture. Tests : `tests/test_manual_review.py`.
* **Companion 1.0.28 — « Échec de connexion » ne s'affiche plus pour un jeton webhook vide ([#37](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/37))** — Un jeton vide était annoncé comme une panne réseau, et Tester pouvait écraser un jeton déjà enregistré. Le panneau demande maintenant de coller le jeton (MetaKavita → Configuration).

## [1.7.0] - 2026-08-13 (Library Inventory, per-volume metadata, and a hardening campaign on Kavita writes, provider pacing and manual review)

EN
### ⚠️ Good to know
This release gathers everything that changed since **1.6.5**: version 1.6.6 was prepared, run on the development branch and never published, so its **Library Inventory**, its redrawn modals and its forty-odd fixes arrive here rather than in a release of their own.
* **The community catalogue ships with this image.** The scrapers your container runs come from two sources: the community catalogue first, the image only to fill the gaps. MetaKavita now compares their versions and refuses to replace a scraper with an older copy. The catalogue has been rebuilt from this release — ComicVine, Bédéthèque, Planète BD, MangaDex and Manga-News included — so a normal boot will not write `Downgrade core refused` lines. If one appears anyway, it is still the protection working: the copy already installed stays. Nothing to do on your side.
* **Your “Comic” libraries change provider cascade.** Kavita shows two library types that read almost the same: *Comic (Flexible)* and *Comic*. MetaKavita had them the wrong way round, and on top of that filed *Image* libraries with the books and *Light Novel* with the manga — so an image library was asked of Google Books and a light novel was treated as a manga. All four are now read as Kavita names them: *Comic* follows the strict cascade (ComicVine first), *Comic (Flexible)* the hybrid comic-then-manga cascade, an image library the manga cascade, and a light novel the book cascade. **What this means concretely: after the update, those libraries no longer ask the same providers in the same order.** That is the point — until now half of them were being treated as their opposite — but if you have set a provider order per library type in the **Providers** modal, read it again before your first batch. Metadata already written in Kavita is untouched.
* **Two options are gone, because they promised something impossible.** *Auto reading direction — option removed.* That tick box promised to set your series' reading direction for you (right-to-left for manga) when writing metadata into Kavita. It could never set anything: in Kavita the reading direction does not belong to the series, it belongs to each reader, and nothing lets you impose it series by series from the outside. To read your manga right to left, set it once and for all in your Kavita account preferences — the choice then applies to everything you read. The **Reading direction** box has also gone from the targeted fields, in the sidebar as well as in a series' options, for the same reason. Your existing settings do not change: if you had unticked boxes, the others are honoured exactly as before, and a series that targeted only this one still writes nothing.

### ✨ What's new
* **C69. Metadata for each volume and each album ([#27](https://github.com/raukorim-bot/MetaKavita/issues/27))** — Until now MetaKavita wrote the series and left every volume as your files had named them: `Tome 07`, no summary, no date, and a cover cut from the first page. The Inventory told you what was missing; this fills what is there. Open a series' volume report and **Enrich volumes** shows what would be written, volume by volume: one row per volume, one column per field, the current value struck through where it would be replaced, and a tick box on every row. Nothing is sent until you press the button. What gets written in this first version is the **album title, the summary, the release date, the ISBN and the cover** — and only into fields that are empty. A field you filled by hand, or locked in Kavita, is left alone and says why in the preview, so an untouched column reads as a decision rather than a failure.
  * **It ships switched off, and it says it is experimental.** The whole feature is off by default (`VOLUME_ENRICHMENT_ENABLED`) and its API answers 403 while it is — a tab left open cannot start writing to your library. An **Experimental** tag sits next to its title in the sidebar, in the toolbar, in the help modal and at the top of the volume report, because it writes into Kavita from whatever a provider chose to list: read the preview before trusting it, and expect the behaviour to change. A danger-block switch, **Overwrite volume metadata** (`VOLUME_FORCE_OVERWRITE`), lifts the fill-the-blanks rule for a run, for when you change provider and want the new source to win.
  * **The pass takes the series you tick.** It sits next to Analyze, with the same progress bar and the same Cancel, and it works on the very selection the scraping batch already uses — same checkboxes, same **Select all** when you do want the whole library. Clicking with nothing ticked writes nothing and says so on the button, and the confirmation names the number of series it is about to write to, since a number is the only thing that catches an unintended selection before it becomes writes.
  * **Where the data comes from.** **ComicVine** returns a hundred issues per request, so a 150-issue run costs two calls; **Bédéthèque** and **Planète BD** walk their album lists page by page, at the pace their sites tolerate; and volumes that carry an ISBN are looked up on **Google Books**, then Open Library, then Hardcover. Manga are the hard case — most scanned manga carry no ISBN. **Manga-News** now lists the French volumes of a series (album title, summary, ISBN, release date), one page per volume, at six seconds between pages and a ceiling of forty volumes, so a long series stays slow rather than getting the site to block you. **MangaDex** still returns the real cover of every volume, one request for the whole series; those covers no longer stop the cascade, so Manga-News can still be asked afterwards. For volumes with no ISBN at all and no Manga-News hit, an experimental switch (**Search by title and number**) looks each one up on Google Books by series title plus volume number. It stays a search, so every candidate's title must contain the series name *and* announce the volume number before it is written — `Berserk, Vol. 7` passes, `Bleach, Vol. 7` and `Berserk Illustrations File` do not.
  * **You choose who answers.** The pass keeps the first provider that recognises a series and writes all of its volumes from that one answer, so the order it asks in decides everything: it follows your **Providers** cascade, per library type, the same order series enrichment uses. On a manga library, **Manga-News** is asked first all the same — it is the only index that returns a title, a summary, an ISBN and a date; MangaDex stays as a fallback for covers, and the series cascade is untouched. On a **Comic (Flexible)** library the comics stay first, and Manga-News is the manga last resort rather than MangaDex. Forcing a provider still wins. Two settings narrow it further. **No manga fallback on Comic (Flexible)** drops the providers that only serve manga on Kavita's hybrid library type, where searching Manga-News for “Gaston Lagaffe” is pure loss. **Force a provider for volumes** asks one and one only; its menu separates the two families, because they do not return the same thing — a provider that lists a whole series in one call, or one that identifies a single volume from its ISBN and returns nothing without one. Naming **Google Books** there also opens its title-and-number search, with no need for the experimental switch: asking for one provider by name is that same permission, and forcing it drops the series index in the same move — so requiring the switch as well returned an empty preview to the very gesture meant to fill one, which is what happens on every scanned manga. Open Library and Hardcover cannot search that way; forcing them on volumes with no ISBN returns nothing, and the log says which of the two it is rather than letting it read as a series nobody knows. A forced provider is honoured only where it can serve the library, so forcing a comic provider does not leave your manga libraries with no provider at all.
  * **What it deliberately leaves alone.** Specials and loose chapters: their numbering means nothing to a provider, and matching them by number would write volume 1's data onto a spin-off. One-shots too — a single file has no volume number — unless they carry an **ISBN** in Kavita, which identifies an edition and is the surer of the two keys; a one-shot with neither is set aside before the first call, and the preview says which case it is and how to fix it rather than blaming the provider. Two files that turn out to be the same album are flagged as duplicates, so you can untick one instead of having both written in silence. And what it does not write, it carries over: a chapter write replaces the whole chapter in Kavita, so the payload rebuilds it from the state read a moment earlier — the AniList, MyAnimeList, Hardcover, Metron, ComicVine, MangaBaka and CBR ids that tie a volume to its Kavita+ entry included, along with everything else MetaKavita has no business touching.
  * **Author credits are a separate switch** (**Volume credits**), off by default, because they cost one extra request per album. Unlike a series write, a chapter write consults no lock on Kavita's side, so a category of credits you locked or already filled is protected here by MetaKavita itself.
  * Tests: `tests/test_volume_enrichment_end_to_end.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_plan.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_job.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_manga.py`, `tests/test_volume_enrichment_oneshot.py`, `tests/test_volume_provider_forcing.py`, `tests/test_kavita_chapter_payload.py`, `tests/test_scraper_bd_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_scraper_comicvine_volume_index.py`, `tests/test_scraper_manganews_volume_index.py`.
* **C66. Library Inventory — what is missing from your library (precursor to #27)** — A new full-width **Inventory** panel answers the question the dashboard never could: which series are incomplete. **Analyze library** runs in the background — it reads your volumes and chapters from Kavita, asks the provider cascade (AniList / MAL / ComicVine, then the backup scrapers) how many there should be, and clusters look-alike series. You get a **Library health** bar (complete / incomplete / expected unknown) with counts and the age of the last analysis, chips for **Missing / Duplicates / No id**, plus **Quick analyze** — which skips the throttled provider calls for series that have not moved since the last pass — and **Cancel**, because a full analysis of a large library takes a while. Each series row carries an `N/M` badge coloured by how complete it is: green when you have everything, cyan when you are up to date on a series still being published, amber then orange then red as it degrades, purple when you own more than the catalogue expects, grey when no catalogue knows the series. A dashed outline marks a count you **forced by hand**, and every forced count is listed in one place with one-click release. **Chapters count as their own unit** (`8/32 ch`) for series filed without volumes. Open a series' report to see stat cards, the missing numbers folded into ranges (`2–13, 18` rather than twelve numbers), the volume-by-volume detail, the forced-expected field, and an **Exclude from inventory** switch for series no catalogue will ever know — they keep being scraped, they just stop skewing the counters. An isolated number far beyond the expected count — an omnibus that Kavita numbered `101` on a 17-issue series — is set aside as **out of range** and named, instead of counting as one more volume and inventing eighty missing numbers. The **Missing details** modal exports to CSV / TXT with the unit stated. Duplicates are scored on both directions of the comparison with edition / spin-off markers, judged at a threshold you set inside the modal, then dismissed or deleted from Kavita. The whole feature switches off from the sidebar (**Library inventory**, `LIBRARY_INVENTORY_ENABLED`): panel, badges and report buttons disappear and the API answers 403, so a tab left open cannot start an analysis. Works across **all libraries** at once. It still only reads — no volume metadata is written and nothing is merged yet. Tests: `tests/test_library_audit.py`, `tests/test_dashboard_renders.py`.
  * A series whose analysis could not conclude — Kavita unavailable for a moment — keeps its previous verdict and gets a hatched segment of its own, **Not analyzed**, counted alongside the others: the bar's segments add up to the number of series it announces, and the label only shows when there is something to report. Run the analysis again and those series are picked up.
  * The CSV exports declare every text value as text, so a spreadsheet displays a title that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` instead of evaluating it as a formula — the cell still reads the same. TXT exports are untouched.
* **C67. Modals: one shell, illustrated, responsive down to a phone** — Every modal now sits on the same shell: one scroll area instead of a scrollbar wherever the layout happened to overflow, a real × button, and actions in a footer that stays put while you scroll a long report. Table-heavy modals (Missing details, Duplicates) widen to 1180 px so the columns stop fighting, and under 720 px the tables fold into labelled cards rather than scrolling sideways — on a phone each modal becomes a full-screen sheet. The cover picker widened to 920 px, because comparing three covers per row is comparing blind. The duplicates modal became a list of aligned rows (title on the left, Kavita and Delete on the right) with the group's score and reasons as chips. Report cards and review-recap tiles line up in equal columns instead of leaving holes. And the modals are finally drawn: every title carries a line-art icon tinted like its header, and empty, loading and error states are an illustration with a title and one line of explanation — a full shelf and a tick for *nothing missing*, a magnifier for *not analysed yet*, an empty crate for *nothing returned*, a warning triangle on failure, a spinning arc while loading (still, if you asked for reduced motion).
* **C68. The statistics card, readable at a glance** — The five figures sat side by side and wrapped after the fourth, leaving *Ignored* alone on a line of its own. They become a legend instead: one line per state, carrying the colour of its segment in the bar, its count and its share of the library, with the states at zero kept on screen but stepped back. The card opens on the number of series tracked, so the percentages have something to hang on. **A sixth state joins them — *Needs seal***, metadata written but Kavita's locks not set, which a file scan can overwrite: the card never showed it although the total counted it, so the figures never quite added up. They do now. Clicking a line filters the series list on that state, and clicking *Ignored* also unhides them, since they are hidden by default. Tests: `tests/test_sidebar_stats_card.py`.
* **C65. Manual covers protected out in the open (🔒 chip + mass escape hatch)** — Protecting a hand-picked cover used to mean silently unticking `cover` in that series' targeted fields: an invisible edit to your own configuration that you could only undo by clicking. Provenance is now tracked on its own, so **your targeted fields are never rewritten behind your back**. The dashboard row shows a clickable **🔒 Manual cover** chip — one click hands the cover back to automatic management — and a new sidebar switch **Overwrite manual covers** (`COVER_FORCE_OVERWRITE`, in the danger block next to Force update) lifts the protection for a whole run: tick it once to rewrite covers after switching provider, instead of clicking series by series. An automatic overwrite drops the mark (the cover belongs to the provider again); an explicit pick, from the Covers modal or a review, always wins and marks it again. Kavita's own cover lock cannot play this role: MetaKavita has to set it on *every* upload, otherwise a Kavita scan regenerates the thumbnail from the files. Tests: `tests/test_cover_manual_protection.py`, `tests/test_routes_series_cover.py`.
* **Magic Input for cover search** — The cover picker now honours a series' Magic URL or ID: the resolved provider is asked for that exact series instead of searching by title. Tests: `tests/test_magic_cover_search.py`.
* **C80. A light mode: the sidebar can now be reduced to what you actually use** — Three families of settings are of no use to a good half of installations: **manual review**, the **Inventory**, and **volume enrichment**. Their switches were unavoidable all the same, in a sidebar where they take up as much room as everything else put together, and where two of the five categories carry an experimental badge nobody asked to be offered. The **Light mode** section of the configuration modal now lets each of the three be removed from the sidebar options. Hiding one switches its feature off in the same move — that is deliberate, and it is said in the modal rather than left to be discovered: a feature whose settings have left the screen is a feature nothing commands any more, and two of these three write to Kavita. Hiding manual review therefore also empties the review queue, as unticking the mode itself has always done, so no series stays frozen waiting for a review that can no longer be reached. Ticking a section back brings it in as it left, switched off, and you decide from there. The rule holds wherever the state comes from — a `config.json` edited by hand or an environment variable claiming a hidden section with its feature on is settled the same way, since that is precisely the case where you would have no switch left to get yourself out of it. Tests: `tests/test_ui_light_mode.py`.
* **C81. Companion is downloadable from the page, and the top bar has two menus instead of one catch-all** — The Companion — the extension that puts **Super Review**, **Auto** and **Config** buttons straight onto Kavita's series pages — is sideloaded from two zips on GitHub, and the only thing pointing at it was a documentation link in the middle of the Help menu. A card now sits under the top bar with both archives, **Chrome / Edge** and **Firefox**, side by side and of equal weight — neither is the recommended path, it is whichever browser you already have — plus the install-and-pairing guide, because two zips without the procedure are of no use. It closes with a cross, and the closing holds from one page load to the next. It is remembered by your browser rather than by the server, for two reasons: the server has no way of knowing whether you installed the extension, and a card of this kind does not deserve a configuration key of its own. The cross is not a one-way door either — **Help → Download Companion** brings the card back, with a brief ring around it since it reappears above whatever you were reading.
  * **Scrapers have their own menu.** The single Help menu mixed what you read with what you do: the user guide and the roadmap next to *Manage scrapers*, the store and the setup assistant. Installing a source has nothing to do with reading a page. **Scrapers** now sits next to **Help** and holds the three things that act — installed scrapers, the store, the provider cascade — followed by the community repository and the scraper-writing guide. Help keeps About, the release notes, the setup assistant, the documentation, the Companion card and, at the bottom, scraper diagnostics.
  * **Both buttons now look like menus.** Nothing said they opened anything: an emoji and a word, in a bar where every other button acts immediately. They carry an icon, a chevron that turns over when the panel is open, and a highlighted state while it is. Opening one closes the other, which two panels anchored in the same corner require. Menu entries carry a picture, and an arrow marks the ones that leave the site for GitHub. The pictures are drawn as strokes in the shared sprite, so they follow the theme and its states without redeclaring anything; the sprite is now read at the top of the page, since `<use>` cannot resolve a symbol declared below it before the whole document has been parsed — the bar is painted with its icons rather than a moment later. The version chip, Config and the theme toggle follow the same vocabulary; the two support pills keep their emojis, they are not part of the tooling. Tests: `tests/test_ui_topbar_companion.py`.
* **C82. The release notes are readable again — one language, the latest version, a title per entry** — This window is the only place the project explains what it just changed, and it was working against itself: it printed the **English and French versions of every release one after the other**, seventeen releases deep, in a single flat list where each entry was a four-hundred-word paragraph with its title buried inside it. Nobody reads a wall like that, which means the work it describes went unread too. It now shows **your language only**, the newest release **open** and the previous ones folded away one line each. Every section is recognisable at a glance — before you update, what's new, fixes, known limitations, each with its own colour, its icon and the number of entries it holds — and every entry leads with its title on its own line, with its code (`C82`, `BF148`) as a chip you can quote in an issue, so you can skim the list and read only what concerns you. The paragraphs themselves are set to a reading width instead of the modal's full span, and the modal is wider, because forty-five characters a line is what turned these texts into columns. The notes are also written differently from now on: **what's new before the fixes**, and nothing about a bug that never reached a released version — a defect introduced and repaired inside the same release has no reader, and listing it made the news look like a repair log. Tests: `tests/test_changelog_render.py`.
* **C83. The mass-action bar reads like a bar of actions again** — Its six buttons were declared to share the width equally, in spaced-out capitals: half the labels broke onto two lines (*Ignore the / selection*, *Stop (cancel the / queue)*), the Inventory tick box sat bare between two blocks, and the one button that writes to your library looked exactly like an error amnesty. Width is no longer the hierarchy. Maintenance gestures — amnesty, ignore, open the queue — are outlined and sit on the left; **Run selection** is the only filled button and sits on the right, next to a **Stop** that stays quiet until a batch is actually running, then turns solid red. Each button is as wide as its own label, on one line, with a stroke icon from the shared sprite instead of an emoji, and the primary button's icon follows what it will do: a triangle to start a batch, a plus when your selection would join one already running. The Inventory option becomes the same pill switch the sidebar uses, and its tooltip explains the option instead of repeating its label. On a narrow window the bar stacks, keeping the run button twice the width of the stop button — and one full-width column each on a phone. Tests: `tests/test_ui_batch_actions.py`.
* **C73. The scraper store can no longer hand you a scraper your version cannot run** — A store scraper does not run beside MetaKavita, it runs inside it: it calls the methods and helpers of the version you have installed. So a scraper written against a newer release fails at *import* on an older one — and the registry, which catches per scraper, logs a line and unbinds it. The provider simply vanishes from every search, with nothing on screen to explain it. The catalogue is shared by every version in service, which makes it the only place that can know: an entry may now declare the MetaKavita version it needs, and both the automatic sync and the manual install honour it — the working copy is kept, the log names the version required, and the store neither offers an update it would refuse nor lets you click Install for nothing. Until now the only protection was the maintainer remembering not to publish, which is one command away from being forgotten. Tests: `tests/test_scraper_manage_store.py`.
  * **Core scrapers now carry a version, and an older copy no longer replaces a newer one.** The scrapers your container runs are the ones on disk, written by two sources — the community catalogue first, the image only to fill the gaps — and the only comparison between them was a checksum, which says that two copies differ but never which one is newer. A provider fixed in a newer image could therefore be held back for good by a catalogue that lags a release by design. Versions are compared now, a downgrade is refused, and every refusal is logged naming both versions.

### 🐛 Bug Fixes
* **BF104. WebLinks replace on force + clear context** — With **Clear Kavita context when forced** + forced update, targeted WebLinks no longer append onto stale Kavita URLs (false-positive leftovers). Links are replaced by this scrape’s set (or cleared if none). Merge/append unchanged when the reset-context option is off. Help text / README updated. Tests: `tests/test_weblinks_reset_context.py`.
* **BF105. Super/Manual Review: hide below-threshold while streaming** — Live candidates under the match threshold no longer flash in before an above-threshold hit arrives. Persistent checkbox **Show below threshold** (`localStorage`); hint when only weak results are hidden; empty-above fallback after scrape completes; orphan master/source selection cleared when weak cards are hidden.
* **BF106. A sync no longer wipes a manually picked cover** — MetaKavita left `coverImageLocked` out of the series update it sends to Kavita, and Kavita read that absence as "the user unlocked the cover": it emptied the cover and scheduled a regeneration from the files. Since a hand-picked cover was also protected from being re-uploaded, the result was a cover destroyed and replaced by a thumbnail cut from the first page. The lock (and the *don't match* flag) are now carried over from Kavita's current state in both update passes. Tests: `tests/test_kavita_api.py`.
* **BF107. Companion Super Review now offers the cover picker** — A Super Review started from the Companion read its options from the dashboard sidebar, which does not exist inside the embedded window: the cover phase was therefore skipped every time, even with **Cover pick** enabled. The embed now receives the server's own settings, and cover previews inside it render without needing a session. Tests: `tests/test_companion_embed_auth.py`.
* **BF108. A silent Kavita library no longer erases your hand-made settings** — Loading the dashboard cleans out series that no longer exist in Kavita. When one library answered with a timeout or a 500, its series were simply absent from the inventory — and treated as deleted: forced id, targeted fields, publisher preference, manual cover mark and any pending review for those series were dropped, with nothing to warn you. The cleanup now requires an inventory read in full, from every library, and says in the live log when it stands down. An empty inventory never purges anything. Tests: `tests/test_orphan_cache_guard.py`.
* **BF109. The Sync button no longer depends on how long the sync takes** — Clicking **Update** on a row ran the whole enrichment inside the HTTP request: a few seconds to several minutes, during which a reverse proxy was free to cut the connection and show you a failure on a series that had in fact been processed. The click now puts the series at the head of the queue, ahead of a running batch, without touching the batch you had queued yourself — including a paused one, whose entries live only in the database. The button is released by an end-of-job signal, so a series parked for manual review reads as a success and an unreachable Kavita releases it at once instead of leaving the row spinning. Two syncs of the same series can no longer overlap either. Tests: `tests/test_force_sync_queued.py`, `tests/test_sync_worker_resilience.py`.
* **BF110. Cover search respects provider rate limits** — Opening the cover picker fires one request per provider, in parallel, and those requests ignored the pacing that enrichment carefully applies: searching for a cover during a batch doubled the traffic sent to each API, which is how a 429 — then an IP ban — arrives. Both paths now share the same pacing per provider. Tests: `tests/test_cover_search_throttle.py`.
* **BF111. A Companion window only ever sees its own series** — The short-lived token the extension uses is issued for one series, from that series' Kavita page, but on the review routes it was accepted for any series: the review queue of the whole library could be read through it, and another series' review confirmed or skipped. Every review route and the live channel now check the series against the token, and mass operations (bulk accept, purge) are refused outright. Tests: `tests/test_companion_embed_auth.py`, `tests/test_socket_handlers.py`.
* **BF112. Signing in during a batch no longer fails** — The accounts table lives in the same file as the cache, but the login path opened it without the settings the rest of the app uses: a login or a password change attempted while a batch or a library analysis was writing could come back as *database is locked*. Both paths now open the database the same way. Tests: `tests/test_auth_db_connection.py`.
* **BF114. One failing series no longer stops every synchronisation** — All syncing goes through a single background worker. An unexpected error outside the enrichment's own safety net — the database momentarily locked, a live-channel emit failing, a missing translation — did not just lose that series: it ended the worker. From then on nothing was processed at all, the batch bar stayed frozen mid-run, and Kavita events and automatic checks piled up in a queue no one was reading, until the container was restarted. Nothing said so. A series that fails is now abandoned on its own, with its reason in the log, the batch bar moves on, and the queue keeps draining. The automatic check survives an unreadable configuration the same way. Tests: `tests/test_sync_worker_resilience.py`.
* **BF113. Labels that stayed in English** — Five strings — the row's **Options** button and its progress label, the cover step's two keyboard hints, the Companion timeout message — were never handed to the interface's dictionary, so they showed in English whatever language you had chosen; the row result labels were written straight into the code. All of them go through the dictionary now. Tests: `tests/test_frontend_i18n_and_escaping.py`.
* **BF121. A manual review no longer lowers the match threshold for the whole app** — Opening a Manual or Super Review drops the provider acceptance threshold to zero on purpose, so you get to judge the weak matches instead of losing them. That drop was applied process-wide: an automatic enrichment running at the same time on *another* series accepted its very first candidate whatever its score, and wrote a stranger's summary, cover and authors into Kavita without a word. Worse, two overlapping collections left the threshold at zero for good, until the container was restarted — from then on every series was enriched with the first thing any provider returned. The drop is now scoped to the collection that asked for it, and nests safely; the parallel scrapers of that collection still inherit it. Tests: `tests/test_match_threshold_portee.py`.
* **BF122. Writing external ids no longer wipes a manually picked cover** — Same cause as BF106, in the call that stores the AniList / MyAnimeList / MangaBaka ids: it left the cover lock out of the payload, and Kavita read that absence as an unlock — it emptied the cover and scheduled a regeneration from the files. That call goes out first, before the cover step, which is precisely the step skipped for a hand-picked cover: the cover was destroyed with nothing put back. The lock and the *don't match* flag are now carried over from Kavita's current state, as they already were in the two other update paths. Tests: `tests/test_external_ids_write.py`.
* **BF123. Weak candidates are no longer announced as strong ones** — Cards streamed into the review modal while the scrapers were still running were all filed above the threshold, badge-free and confirmation-free, even at a score of 0.20 — the band was computed with the lowered collection threshold instead of your real one. It was also stored that way, so a scrape that never finished (crash, restart) left the queue full of false matches that **Accept all** would have applied in bulk. The band now always uses your threshold, and the streamed cards agree with the final result. Tests: `tests/test_match_threshold_portee.py`.
* **BF124. A refused external-id write no longer reads as a success** — Kavita's answer to the AniList / MyAnimeList / MangaBaka id write was never read: the series went green as *completed* with those fields still empty, and since that status is terminal nothing ever tried again. The refusal is now logged and the series is not marked completed — everything that did get written stays written. Re-run the series in forced mode to retry the ids. Tests: `tests/test_external_ids_write.py`.
* **BF125. The end of a scrape no longer undoes what you just did** — Candidates are collected while the review modal is already open and usable, and the end of that collection rebuilt the review from scratch. Skipping a review during the scrape made it vanish and then come back a few seconds later; picking a provider during the scrape sent the review back to the picking step, losing the preview and the choice. The end of a scrape now only refreshes the list of candidates: it never resurrects a review you passed on, and never walks back a choice you made. Tests: `tests/test_manual_review_finalize_etat.py`.
* **BF126. A re-scrape no longer destroys the review you are examining** — A batch launched on an explicit selection, or a plain Kavita event, went over a series already parked for manual review: starting a review replaces the one that series already had, and in manual mode that replacement happens before anything is even scraped. The open modal was left pointing at a review that no longer existed — *review not found* on confirm, and all the work to redo. A series in review is no longer re-scraped by a non-forced pass, and the Kavita summary no longer enters that decision (a series is in review precisely because it has none). Re-running the scrape on purpose — the row button, forced mode, the Companion — still replaces the review, and a replacement now leaves a trace in the log. Tests: `tests/test_pending_review_not_clobbered.py`.
* **BF128. MangaUpdates: a validated match is no longer lost when the detail call fails** — The search already builds the full series record; a second request fetches the detailed version, and the API sits behind Cloudflare. A 403, a 429 or a dropped connection on that second request threw everything away: the log announced *match validated (87%)* and the provider then contributed nothing. The record from the search is now used as a fallback, the detail still taking precedence when it answers. Tests: `tests/test_scraper_mangaupdates_detail_fallback.py`.
* **BF129. Manual review: the right arrow brings back the series as you left it** — Moving to the next series with the keyboard dropped you back on the candidate list even for a series whose provider you had already picked: everything had to be done again.
